   192	                    if a==b{continue;}
   193	                    if !matches!((paired::observe(e,p,viewer,args.clone()),paired::observe(&mut absent,p,viewer,args.clone())),(Ok(aa),Ok(bb)) if a==aa&&b==bb){out.unsupported.insert(format!("{} / {}: paired history observation is unstable or unavailable on repeat",p.name,inv.target_label()));continue;}
   194	                    let added=violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,None,initial,"read");
   195	                    if added&&let Some(v)=out.violations.last_mut(){v.operation=p.name.clone();v.args=args;v.observations=Some(paired::Observations{present:a,absent:b,absent_rows:absent_rows.clone()});}
   196	                    continue 'actors;
   197	                }
   198	            }
   199	        }}
   200	    }
   201	}
   202	fn prior_policy(bound:&Bound,inv:&Invariant,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,old:Option<&Value>,row:&Value,prior:&mut Option<Engine>)->Result<bool,String>{
   203	    match prior.as_mut(){Some(e)=>policy_in(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row,e),None=>policy(bound,inv,rows,viewer,old,row)}
   204	}
   205	fn assertions(bound:&Bound,before:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,after:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,steps:&[Step],prior:&mut Option<Engine>,current:&mut Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
   206	    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.target=="always"){
   207	        for row in after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten(){
   208	            let old=before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().find(|r|r.field("id").ok()==row.field("id").ok());
   209	            if policy_in(bound,inv,after,viewer,old,row,current)!=Ok(false){continue;}
   210	            // A pre-existing seed defect is supplemental; a newly violated
   211	            // assertion or changed image is an authored committed observation.
   212	            if old!=Some(row)||old.is_some_and(|r|prior_policy(bound,inv,before,viewer,Some(r),r,prior)==Ok(true)){
   213	                violation(out,inv,steps,viewer,row,old,initial,"always");
   214	            }
   215	        }
   216	    }
   217	}
   218	fn changed(bound:&Bound,_p:&Program,before:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,after:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,steps:&[Step],prior:&mut Option<Engine>,current:&mut Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
   219	    assertions(bound,before,after,initial,viewer,steps,prior,current,out);
   220	    // Disappearance is coverage evidence only. Actual delete authority is
   221	    // judged by the executed-write trace, never by a commit-end image diff.
   222	    for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|matches!(i.target.as_str(),"delete"|"write")){
   223	        if bound.schema.tables[&inv.table].is_expiring()&&before.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|old|!after.get(&inv.table).into_iter().flatten().any(|new|new.field("id").ok()==old.field("id").ok())){
   224	            out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: implicit retirement is not an executed delete; delete intent requires an authored write boundary",inv.target_label()));
   225	        }
   226	    }
   227	}
   228	
   229	fn role_candidates<'a>(bound:&Bound,p:&'a Program,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,actors:&[String],cases:&[BTreeMap<String,Value>],fixture:&Engine,campaign:&mut Campaign)->Vec<(&'a Program,String,BTreeMap<String,Value>)>{
   230	    let mut out=Vec::new();let mut groups=Vec::new();
   231	    let json=serde_json::to_string(p).unwrap();
   232	    if !ir::write_tables(p).iter().any(|(table,_)|!super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,table).is_empty()){return out;}
   233	    if !campaign.reserve(1){return Vec::new();}
   234	    let Ok(mut judge)=fork(bound,fixture,Vec::new())else{return Vec::new();};
   235	    for (table,_) in ir::write_tables(p){
//! Repairs lead; uncertainty stays visible without repeating every proof blocker.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#144-actions-precede-coverage-details — advice is separate from replayed failures.
use super::{Report, Status};

/// One consequence ordering shared by check and audit. Lower means more urgent.
pub fn note_priority(code: &str) -> u8 {
    match code {
        "N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT" | "N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE" => 0,
        "N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION" | "N_SELF_CLAIMED_RELATIONSHIP" => 1,
        "N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE" | "N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE" | "N_ROLE_WIDE_READ" |
        "N_BROAD_READ" | "N_CALLER_TIME" | "N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE" |
        "N_CREATION_REASON" | "N_CREATION_MONEY" | "N_CONSTANT_APPROVAL" |
        "N_TRANSITION_BYPASS" | "N_STALE_CREATION_CHECK" | "N_DURATION_BOUNDS" |
        "N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE" => 2,
        "N_COLUMN_RESULT_EXPOSURE" | "N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT" => 5,
        "N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE" => 6,
        "N_SECURITY_INTENT" => 7,
        _ => 4,
    }
}

impl Report {
    /// Full structured evidence remains in the report; this is a human action list.
    pub fn action_text(&self, limit: usize) -> String {
        let failures = self.invariants.iter().filter(|v| v.status == Status::Refuted &&
            v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c| c.reachability.is_reachable())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let mut notes = self.notes.iter().collect::<Vec<_>>();
        notes.sort_by_key(|n| (note_priority(&n.code), n.site.file.clone(), n.site.line, n.site.col, &n.code, &n.message));
        notes.dedup_by(|a,b| a.site == b.site && a.code == b.code && a.message == b.message);
        let mut out = format!("security actions: {} fix{}, {} review{}\n", failures.len(),
            if failures.len() == 1 { "" } else { "es" }, notes.len(), if notes.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" });
        for v in &failures {
            let c = v.counterexample.as_ref().unwrap();
            out.push_str(&format!("  FIX {}: {} via {} as {} ({}); tighten enforcement to the declared intent, then run snapback4 test\n",
                v.invariant.site, v.invariant.target_label(), c.operation, c.viewer, serde_json::to_value(&c.reachability).unwrap().as_str().unwrap()));
        }
        let mut shown = 0;
        for note in notes.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(i,n)|
            (i < limit || note_priority(&n.code) <= 2).then_some(*n)) {
            shown += 1;
            out.push_str(&format!("  REVIEW {} {}: {}\n    {}\n",note.site,note.code,note.message,note.rewrite));
        }
        if shown < notes.len() { out.push_str(&format!("  {} more reviews: snapback4 audit --all or check --verbose\n", notes.len()-shown)); }
        let proved = self.invariants.iter().filter(|v| v.status == Status::Proved).count();
        let refuted = self.invariants.iter().filter(|v|v.status==Status::Refuted).count();
        let unproved = self.invariants.len()-proved-refuted;
        out.push_str(&format!("security: {proved} proved, {refuted} refuted, {unproved} unproved ({} failing)\n", failures.len()));
        let supplemental = self.invariants.iter().filter(|v| v.status == Status::Refuted &&
            v.counterexample.as_ref().is_some_and(|c| !c.reachability.is_reachable())).count();
        if supplemental > 0 {out.push_str(&format!("  {supplemental} seed/synthetic observations are non-failing\n"));}
        if unproved > 0 || !self.campaign_coverage.is_empty() {
            out.push_str(&format!("  next: snapback4 test; {} coverage gaps (audit --all/JSON keeps every reason)\n",self.campaign_coverage.len()));
        }
        if self.invariants.is_empty() {out.push_str("  next: answer snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft from the spec; declare intent and run snapback4 test\n");}
        out
    }
}
//! Role authority is compared by effective gates, never by names like 'admin'.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#123-actionable-authority-advice — advisory grant-edge analysis, not a security proof or implicit exemption.
use super::*;
pub(super) fn probe_pairs(bound:&Bound,table:&str,index:&str,key:&[RuleTerm],equalities:&[(String,RuleTerm)])->Vec<(String,RuleTerm)>{
    let mut pairs=equalities.to_vec();
    if let Some(idx)=bound.schema.table(table).and_then(|t|t.index(index)){for (component,term) in idx.components.iter().zip(key){if let Some(column)=component.column(){pairs.push((column.into(),term.clone()));}}}
    pairs
}
pub(super) fn global_principal(bound:&Bound,table:&str)->Option<String>{
    let t=bound.schema.table(table)?;
    t.indexes.values().find_map(|index|{
        if !index.unique||index.components.len()!=1{return None;}
        let column=index.components[0].column()?;
        matches!(t.columns.get(column),Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).then(||column.to_string())
    })
}
pub(super) fn membership_probe(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str)->bool{
    let RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..}=rule else{return false;};
    if table!=roster{return false;}
    let Some(principal)=global_principal(bound,table)else{return false;};
    let pairs=probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
    pairs.len()==1&&pairs[0].0==principal&&pairs[0].1==RuleTerm::Viewer
}
// @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — ownership is required for every supplied owned field;
// only an exclusively owned patch bypasses an unnamed row update gate.
fn write_admission(table:&snapback4_core::schema::Table,op:ast::WriteOp,fields:&[ast::ObjectField])->RuleExpr{
    let touched=fields.iter().filter(|f|!f.spread&&f.name!="id").map(|f|f.name.as_str()).collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();
    let owned_only=!touched.is_empty()&&!fields.iter().any(|f|f.spread)&&touched.iter().all(|f|table.written_by.contains_key(*f));
    let gate=match op{
        ast::WriteOp::Insert=>table.rules.insert.clone(),
        ast::WriteOp::Update if owned_only&&!table.update_transition=>RuleExpr::Allow,
        ast::WriteOp::Update=>table.update_rule_judged().into_owned(),
        _=>RuleExpr::Deny,
    };
    let mut owners=table.written_by.iter().filter(|(field,authority)|touched.contains(field.as_str())||op==ast::WriteOp::Update&&!authority.audience&&table.rules.update.needs_field_value(field,&table.columns[*field])).map(|(_,authority)|RuleExpr::Eq(if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{RuleTerm::Field(authority.owner.clone())}else{RuleTerm::OldField(authority.owner.clone())},RuleTerm::Viewer)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    owners.push(RuleExpr::Or(gate.disjuncts().iter().filter(|r|r.service_grant().is_none()).map(|r|(*r).clone()).collect()));
    RuleExpr::And(owners)
}
pub(super) fn membership_grant_risk(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,operation:&str,requirements:&[RuleExpr],op:ast::WriteOp,fields:&[ast::ObjectField])->bool{
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Upsert{return membership_grant_risk(bound,roster,operation,requirements,ast::WriteOp::Insert,fields)||membership_grant_risk(bound,roster,operation,requirements,ast::WriteOp::Update,fields);}
    fn honors(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str)->bool{match rule{
        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|r|honors(bound,r,roster)),
        r=>membership_probe(bound,r,roster),
    }}
    if global_principal(bound,roster).is_none()||!bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|super::columns::rules(t).any(|r|honors(bound,r,roster))){return false;}
    let table=&bound.schema.tables[roster];
    let admission=RuleExpr::And(std::iter::once(write_admission(table,op,fields)).chain(requirements.iter().cloned()).collect());
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Update&&global_principal(bound,roster).is_some_and(|column|kernel::implies(&admission,&RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::OldField(column.clone()),RuleTerm::Field(column)))==Ok(true)){return false;}
    let ordinary=substitute(bound,&admission,"$no-roster","$membership",None,None);
    bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==roster&&i.column.is_none()&&(i.operation.as_deref()==Some(operation)||i.operation.is_none()&&(i.target=="write"||i.target==if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{"insert"}else{"update"}))).filter_map(|i|i.policy.as_ref()).any(|p|super::operation::safe(p)&&kernel::implies(&ordinary,p)==Ok(false))
}
fn substitute(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str,column:&str,role:Option<&str>,target:Option<&str>)->RuleExpr{
    let known=|t:&RuleTerm|match t{RuleTerm::Field(c)|RuleTerm::NextField(c) if c==column=>target.map(|s|snapback4_core::Value::String(s.into())),RuleTerm::Literal(v)=>Some(v.clone()),_=>None};
    match rule{
        RuleExpr::Exists{table,key,index,equalities,..} if table==roster&&key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(|k|matches!(k,RuleTerm::Viewer))=>{
            let pairs=probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
            let requested=pairs.iter().find(|(c,_)|c==column).and_then(|(_,t)|if let RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(s))=t{Some(s.as_str())}else{None});
            if requested.is_none(){rule.clone()}else if requested==role{
                if pairs.iter().all(|(c,t)|c==column||matches!(t,RuleTerm::Viewer)){RuleExpr::Allow}else{rule.clone()}
            }else{RuleExpr::Deny}
        },
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Id(seat)))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Id(seat)),RuleTerm::Viewer) if seat.starts_with("service:")=>RuleExpr::Deny,
        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b)|RuleExpr::Ne(a,b)=>if let (Some(a),Some(b))=(known(a),known(b)){if (a==b)==matches!(rule,RuleExpr::Eq(..)){RuleExpr::Allow}else{RuleExpr::Deny}}else{rule.clone()},
        RuleExpr::And(xs)=>RuleExpr::And(xs.iter().map(|r|substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target)).collect()),
        RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>RuleExpr::Or(xs.iter().map(|r|substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target)).collect()),
        RuleExpr::Not(r)=>RuleExpr::Not(Box::new(substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target))),
        _=>rule.clone(),
    }
}
pub(super) fn risk(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,column:&str,operation:&str,targets:&[String],op:ast::WriteOp,requirements:&[RuleExpr],dynamic:bool,fields:&[ast::ObjectField])->Option<String>{
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Upsert{return risk(bound,roster,column,operation,targets,ast::WriteOp::Insert,requirements,dynamic,fields).or_else(||risk(bound,roster,column,operation,targets,ast::WriteOp::Update,requirements,dynamic,fields));}
    let table=bound.schema.table(roster)?;
    if requirements.iter().any(|r|!r.disjuncts().is_empty()&&r.disjuncts().iter().all(|b|b.service_grant().is_some())){return None;}
    let roles=role_values(bound,roster,column);
    let intents=bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==roster&&i.column.as_deref().is_none_or(|c|c==column)&&(i.operation.as_deref()==Some(operation)||i.operation.is_none()&&(i.target=="write"||i.target==if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{"insert"}else{"update"}))).filter_map(|i|i.policy.as_ref()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    fn role_guards(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,out:&mut Vec<RuleExpr>){match rule{
        RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..} if key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,v)|v)).any(|k|matches!(k,RuleTerm::Viewer))&&probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities).iter().any(|(c,v)|role_columns(bound,table).contains(c)&&matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(_))))=>out.push(rule.clone()),
        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{role_guards(bound,r,out)},_=>{}
    }}
    let admission=RuleExpr::And(std::iter::once(write_admission(table,op,fields)).chain(requirements.iter().cloned()).collect());
    if op==ast::WriteOp::Update&&kernel::implies(&admission,&RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::OldField(column.into()),RuleTerm::Field(column.into())))==Ok(true){return None;}
    let mut guards=Vec::new();role_guards(bound,&admission,&mut guards);
    let independent=kernel::implies(&substitute(bound,&admission,"$no-roster",column,None,None),&RuleExpr::Or(guards))==Ok(true);
    let mut ranked=roles.iter().filter(|r|targets.is_empty()||targets.contains(r)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    let score=|role:&&String|bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(_,t)|super::columns::effects(t)).filter(|(_,rule)|kernel::implies(&RuleExpr::Allow,&substitute(bound,rule,roster,column,Some(role.as_str()),None))==Ok(true)).count();
    ranked.sort_by_key(|role|(std::cmp::Reverse(score(role)),role.as_str()));
    for target in ranked{
        for caller in std::iter::once(None).chain(roles.iter().map(|s|Some(s.as_str()))){
            if caller==Some(target.as_str()){continue;}
            let admitted=substitute(bound,&admission,roster,column,caller,Some(target));
            let admits=kernel::implies(&admitted,&RuleExpr::Deny)!=Ok(true);
            if !admits{continue;}
            let violates_intent=intents.iter().any(|p|kernel::implies(&admitted,&substitute(bound,p,roster,column,caller,Some(target)))==Ok(false));
            // Existing independently gated appointers may delegate different
            // roles. Independent intent can restrict a particular target role.
            if independent&&!violates_intent{continue;}
            if violates_intent{return Some(format!("{roster}.{column} assignment to '{target}' is not established by the independent delegation intent for {}",caller.unwrap_or("a caller without a role")));}
            for (name,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){for (effect,rule) in super::columns::effects(t){
                let granted=substitute(bound,&rule,roster,column,Some(target),None);let previous=substitute(bound,&rule,roster,column,caller,None);
                if kernel::implies(&RuleExpr::Allow,&granted)!=Ok(true){continue;}

@@ -946,3 +946,47 @@ N_COLUMN_READ identifies a possible one-to-one privacy extension. Put a private
 field on its existing row with `read <- policy` when it only narrows payload
 visibility; keep separate entities, lifecycles and write authority in separate
 tables. This advisory never proves equivalence or rewrites stored data.
+
+## Wall ownership: the owner of this wall
+
+A global owner role does not identify which wall a person owns. Store the
+relationship on the wall, copy it into each application under a verified insert
+rule, and keep the audience immutable. A role may still govern staff workflows;
+the signoff below requires this application's wall owner.
+
+```quarry
+use personas alice, bob, artist
+table walls:
+  owner: principal
+  title: text 1..80
+  immutable owner
+  read <- allow
+  insert <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+table wallApplications:
+  wall: walls
+  wallOwner: principal
+  applicant: principal
+  signed: bool default false
+  immutable wall, wallOwner, applicant
+  read <- .applicant = viewer or .wallOwner = viewer
+  insert <- .applicant = viewer and exists walls[.wall, owner = .wallOwner]
+  update <- .wallOwner = viewer
+  online only
+mutation addWall(title: text 1..80):
+  return insert walls {owner:viewer,title}
+mutation applyToWall(wall: walls):
+  w = walls[wall] ! else NOT_FOUND
+  return insert wallApplications {wall,wallOwner:w.owner,applicant:viewer}
+mutation signWall(application: wallApplications):
+  a = wallApplications[application] ! else NOT_FOUND
+  require a.wallOwner = viewer else NOT_WALL_OWNER
+  update wallApplications[application] {signed:true}
+invariant wallApplications.signed update only .wallOwner = viewer
+```
+
+Save this schema as snapback/schema.q in a disposable project; run check and test.
+Alice creates a wall, artist applies, and only Alice signs. Bob owning a different
+wall confers no authority over Alice's application. If walls can transfer owners,
+choose and model whether signoff follows current ownership or the captured owner;
+this recipe deliberately makes ownership immutable.

+
+## Individual security review
+
+Default check puts likely holes before result inventories and restating questions.
+`N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT` and `N_OPTIONAL_UPLOAD_CREATE` require a repair or an
+individual disposition. They fail check without claiming an invariant refutation.
+Read the note, compare it with the specification, then use
+`check --ack-security ID --reason 'specification rationale'` with its current ID
+from check or check --json. Bulk --ack-notes cannot clear these gates.
+Commit snapback/security-reviews.json when the decision should travel with source.
+Every Quarry edit, changed diagnostic or security analyzer revision requires review
+again; the record never grants authority or proves safety. Audit retains the full
+notes and dispositions. Declared refutations still fail and cannot be acknowledged.
+Deploy --strict retains its refutation gate; run check in acceptance before deploy.
+
+Optional upload arguments can skip required evidence even when a conditional child
+insertion has a required media column. Require the upload before creating the
+parent. Reasons use text 1..N when nonempty justification is required; optional
+money and constant payment/approval flags need independent specification review.
+Transition advice asks whether a generic setter can bypass a stricter decision or
+whether eligibility checked at creation must be rechecked at approval. Different
+duration windows on one column may be deliberate: confirm them against the spec.
+Role-wide write questions ask whether a global role may write every resource.
+Model the owner of this particular wall; see guide rule-recipes, wall ownership.
+
+Test explores directed status/eligibility histories up to eight mutations with a
+separate finite successor bound. Check keeps its shared 512-work/1500ms limit.
+Frontier, argument, signature and attempt cuts remain coverage gaps. Synthetic
+observations are REVIEW, while actual admitted histories from validated generated
+fixtures retain their generated-fixture-trace origin and can fail acceptance.
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/upload_recipe.md b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/upload_recipe.md

@@ -1,3 +1,9 @@
+Uploads are bound to the uploader: the account that places an asset must upload
+its bytes. One upload has one home; reusing it in another row refuses. To share
+evidence, reference its original row rather than placing its asset again. Required
+workflow evidence needs a required mutation argument as well as a required column;
+a conditional child insertion does not require evidence on its parent.

   277	/// `check --emit-lean <file.q>`: one file, checked, as the Lean model's
   278	/// schema, helpers and programs (`snapback4_lang::emit::lean`).
   279	// @ref LLP 3000.035#52-stage-2-a-small-lean-core — the emitter's command-line face; the harness calls the library directly
   280	fn emit_lean(file: &str) -> i32 {
   281	    let source = match std::fs::read_to_string(file) {
   282	        Ok(source) => source,
   283	        Err(error) => { eprintln!("cannot read {file}: {error}"); return 2; }
   284	    };
   285	    let name = Path::new(file).file_name().and_then(|n| n.to_str()).unwrap_or(file).to_owned();
   286	    match snapback4_lang::emit::lean::lean(&[(name, source)]) {
   287	        Ok(module) => { println!("{}", serde_json::to_string_pretty(&module.to_json()).unwrap()); 0 }
   288	        Err(snapback4_lang::emit::lean::LeanError::Diagnostics(problems)) => {
   289	            for problem in problems { eprintln!("{problem}"); }
   290	            1
   291	        }
   292	        Err(snapback4_lang::emit::lean::LeanError::Unsupported(u)) => {
   293	            eprintln!("outside the Lean model's fragment: {u}");
   294	            1
   295	        }
   296	    }
   297	}
   298	
   299	pub fn check(options: &Options) -> i32 {
   300	    if options.flags.contains_key("audit") { return security::audit(options); }
   301	    if options.flags.contains_key("store") && options.flags.contains_key("release") {
   302	        return releases::check_release(options);
   303	    }
   304	
   305	    if let Some(file) = options.flags.get("emit-lean") {
   306	        return emit_lean(file);
   307	    }
   308	    let project = project_or_exit();
   309	    let started = Instant::now();
   310	    package_versions::warn(&project);
   311	    upgrade_history::report(&project, options.json);
   312	    if options.flags.contains_key("fix") {
   313	        if let Err(code) = fix::checked(fix::compile(&project)) { return code; }
   314	    }
   315	    let compiled_sources = match project.sources() {
   316	        Ok(sources) => sources,
   317	        Err(error) => { eprintln!("cannot read snapback/: {error}"); return 2; }
   318	    };
   319	    let mut bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&compiled_sources);
   320	    let reviewable_notes = bound.notes.iter().map(note_reviews::fingerprint).collect();
   321	    for warning in saved_fixture_drift::warnings(&project) {
   322	        if warning.drift { eprintln!("check: WARNING: saved fixture drift: {}", warning.message); }
   323	        else { eprintln!("check: info: {}", warning.message); }
   324	    }
   325	    if options.flags.contains_key("fix") && bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
   326	        if let Err(error) = fix_fixtures::run(&project, &bound, options) { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_INPUT", format!("check --fix: {error}")); return 2; }
   327	    }
   328	
   329	    if let Some(hint) = project.saved_copy_hint() { if options.json { eprintln!("check: {hint}"); } else { println!("check: {hint}"); } }
   330	    if let Some(error) = crate::exact_device::inspect(&project.root, &bound.schema).into_iter().find_map(Result::err) {
   331	        if options.json { println!("{}", json!({"ok": false, "denied": {"code": "E_DEVICE_VERSION", "message": error}, "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": []})); }
   332	        else { eprintln!("{error}"); }
   333	        return 1;
   334	    }
   335	    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
   336	        check_surface::report(&project, &bound, options.json);
   337	        report_diagnostics(&bound, options.json, true, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
   338	        if !options.json { print_prediction(&bound, options.flags.contains_key("verbose")); }
   339	        return 1;
   340	    }
   341	    let security = security::report(&project, &bound, &compiled_sources, options);
   342	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#18.3 — check judges adoption against the dev store's active generation and rows, read-only
   343	    match adoption::diagnostics(&project, &bound.schema, options.flags.get("store"), options.json || options.flags.contains_key("verbose")) {
   344	        Ok((problems, notes)) => { bound.diagnostics.extend(problems); bound.notes.extend(notes); },
   345	        Err(error) => { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_STORE", format!("cannot check store adoption: {error}")); return 2; }
   346	    }
   347	    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
   348	        check_surface::report(&project, &bound, options.json);
   349	        report_diagnostics(&bound, options.json, true, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
   350	        if !options.json { print_prediction(&bound, options.flags.contains_key("verbose")); }
   351	        return 1;
   352	    }
   353	    for diagnostic in readiness::notes(&project, &bound, options.flags.get("fixture")) {
   354	        if diagnostic.code == "E_FIXTURE_TIME" { bound.diagnostics.push(diagnostic); }
   355	        else { bound.notes.push(diagnostic); }
   356	    }
   357	    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
   358	        report_diagnostics(&bound, options.json, true, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
   359	        return 1;
   360	    }
   361	    bound.notes.extend(snapback4_lang::bind::residency::change_notes(&bound, &residency::previous(&project)));
   362	    check_surface::report(&project, &bound, options.json);
   363	    if let Err(error) = project.write_generated(&bound) {
   364	        failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_STORE", format!("cannot write generated types: {error}"));
   365	        return 2;
   366	    }
   367	    if let Err(error) = project.check_server() {
   368	        if options.json { println!("{}", json!({ "ok": false, "problems": [{ "code": if error.starts_with("E_SERVER_TOOLCHAIN") { "E_SERVER_TOOLCHAIN" } else { "E_SERVER_TYPECHECK" }, "message": error, "guide": "actions" }], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": [] })); }
   369	        else { eprintln!("{error}"); }
   370	        return 1;
   371	    }
   372	    // A native job's module is built here too (LLP 3000.005): the schema's
   373	    // Rust types written, cargo run, the module checked against the jobs.
   374	    let native = match crate::native::build(&project, &bound) {
   375	        Ok(built) => built,
   376	        Err(problem) => {
   377	            if options.json {
   378	                println!("{}", json!({ "ok": false, "problems": [{ "code": problem.code, "message": problem.message, "rewrite": problem.rewrite, "guide": "native" }], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": [] }));
   379	            } else {
   380	                eprintln!("{problem}\n");
   381	                eprintln!("check: 1 problem");
   382	            }
   383	            return 1;
   384	        }
   385	    };
   386	    check_surface::remember(&project, &bound);
   387	    if let Err(error) = residency::remember(&project, &bound) { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_STORE", format!("cannot write residency facts: {error}")); return 2; }
   388	    let acquisition = if options.flags.contains_key("residency") {
   389	        match residency::at_check(&project, &bound, options) {
   390	            Ok(report) => report,
   391	            Err(error) => { failed_check(&bound, options.json, "E_INPUT", format!("residency measurement failed: {error}")); return 1; }
   392	        }
   393	    } else { None };
   394	    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#132-reviewed-notes — every failed check preserves review evidence, including independent policy refutations.
   395	    let reviewed = if security.refuted() {
   396	        note_reviews::Review { new: bound.notes.clone(), acknowledged: Vec::new() }
   397	    } else { match note_reviews::review(&project, &compiled_sources, &bound.notes, &reviewable_notes, options.flags.contains_key("ack-notes")) {
   398	        Ok(review) => review,
   399	        Err(error) => {
   400	            if options.json { println!("{}", json!({"ok": false, "problems": [{"code": "E_CHECK_NOTES", "message": error}], "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": bound.notes, "acknowledgedNotes": []})); }
   401	            else { eprintln!("check: E_CHECK_NOTES: {error}"); }
   402	            return 2;
   403	        }
   404	    } };
   405	    let dispositions = match security_reviews::review(&project,&compiled_sources,&security.notes,
   406	        if security.refuted(){None}else{options.flags.get("ack-security").map(|id|(id.as_str(),options.flags.get("reason").map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("")))}) {
   407	        Ok(d)=>d,Err(error)=>{if options.json {println!("{}",json!({"ok":false,"problems":[{"code":"E_SECURITY_NOTES","message":error}]}));}else{eprintln!("check: E_SECURITY_NOTES: {error}");}return 2;}
   408	    };
   409	    let security_failed=security.refuted()||security_reviews::pending(&dispositions);
   410	    let visible_security=security.notes.iter().zip(&dispositions).filter(|(_,d)|!d.acknowledged).map(|(n,_)|n).collect::<Vec<_>>();
   411	    let present = |text: &str| crate::presentation::indexes(&bound.schema, text, options.flags.contains_key("verbose"));
   412	    let details = acquisition.is_none() || options.flags.contains_key("verbose");
   413	    let certificates: Vec<_> = bound.programs.iter().map(|p| crate::scale::analyze_bound(&bound.schema, p)).collect();
   414	    let scale_summary = crate::scale::summary(&certificates);
   415	    let note_count = security.notes.len() + reviewed.new.len() + certificates.iter().map(|certificate| certificate.search.len()).sum::<usize>();
   416	    let field_authority: Vec<_> = bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(name,table)| table.written_by.iter().map(move |(column,authority)|
   417	        format!("{name}.{column}: written by .{}; visibility: {}", authority.owner, if authority.audience { "readable by audience" } else { "private to owner" }))).collect();
   418	    let row_views = bound.schema.maintains.iter().filter_map(|m| m.row_key.as_ref().map(|k| json!({
   419	        "view": m.target, "source": m.source, "sourceKey": k.source_key, "member": k.table,
   420	        "memberKey": k.member_key, "fanout": k.fanout, "range": row_view_range(&bound.schema, m), "writerRecount": snapback4_core::engine::row_views::WRITER_RECOUNT, "recountChunk": snapback4_core::engine::row_views::RECOUNT_CHUNK
   421	    }))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
   422	    let elapsed = started.elapsed();
   423	    let synced = bound.schema.synced().count();
   424	    let sequences: Vec<_> = bound.schema.visible_tables().flat_map(|(name, table)| table.sequences.iter().map(move |(column, reference)| {
   425	        format!("sequence {name}.{column}: counter per {} row through .{reference}; immutable, provisional while pending", table.columns[reference])
   426	    })).collect();
   427	    if options.json {
   428	        println!("{}", json!({ "ok": !security_failed, "securityReviews": &dispositions, "security": &security, "followingHistory": bound.programs.iter().filter_map(|p| crate::scale::following::certificate(&bound.schema, &p.program)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "rowViews": row_views, "fieldAuthority": field_authority, "sequences": sequences, "scaleSummary": scale_summary, "search": certificates.iter().flat_map(|certificate| &certificate.search).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "plans": if options.flags.contains_key("cost") { Some(bound.programs.iter().map(|p| (&p.program.name, p.selections())).collect::<BTreeMap<_, _>>()) } else { None }, "scale": if options.flags.contains_key("cost") { Some(&certificates) } else { None }, "tables": bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name, _)| bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_none()).count(), "views": bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name, _)| bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some()).count(), "synced": synced, "operations": bound.programs.len(), "heldQueries": bound.schema.operations.iter().filter(|(_, fact)| fact.held).map(|(name, _)| name).collect::<Vec<_>>(), "notes": bound.notes, "newNotes": reviewed.new, "acknowledgedNotes": reviewed.acknowledged, "predictionSummary": snapback4_lang::bind::residency::prediction_summary(&bound), "starterLeftovers": init::starter_leftovers_json(&project.root), "acquisition": acquisition, "residency": if options.flags.contains_key("residency") { Some(residency::facts(&bound)) } else { None }, "native": native.as_ref().map(|b| json!({ "module": b.path, "bytes": b.bytes, "jobs": b.jobs, "ms": b.ms })), "ms": elapsed.as_millis() }));
   429	    } else {
   430	        println!("check: {} — {} ({synced} synced), {} operations, {}{}, {:.1} ms{}", if security_failed { "failed" } else { "ok" }, table_counts(&bound.schema), bound.programs.len(), if security.refuted() { "security invariant refuted" } else if security_failed { "security review required" } else { "compiled" }, if note_count == 0 { String::new() } else { format!(", {} note{}", note_count, if note_count == 1 { "" } else { "s" }) }, elapsed.as_secs_f64() * 1000.0, match &native { Some(b) => format!("; native: {} → {} ({} B, {} ms)", b.jobs.join(", "), b.path.display(), b.bytes, b.ms), None => String::new() });
   431	        if !reviewed.acknowledged.is_empty() { println!("{} reviewed notes (check --verbose to inspect); source edits require review again", reviewed.acknowledged.len()); }
   432	        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
   433	            for line in &sequences { println!("  {line}"); }
   434	            for (name, _) in bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(_, t)| t.ephemeral.is_some()) {
   435	                println!("  {name}: {}", snapback4_lang::bind::residency::table_line(&bound.schema, name));
   436	            }
   437	            for (name, fact) in bound.schema.operations.iter().filter(|(_, f)| f.ephemeral) {
   438	                println!("{}", present(&snapback4_lang::bind::residency::operation_lines(name, fact)));
   439	            }
   440	        }
   441	        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") { for line in &field_authority { println!("  {line}"); } }
   442	        if options.flags.contains_key("verbose") {
   443	            // Verbose keeps every explanation, in the same consequence order as the summary.
   444	            let mut ranked: Vec<&snapback4_lang::Diagnostic> = bound.notes.iter().collect();
   445	            ranked.sort_by_key(|note| crate::note_rank::tier(&note.code));
   446	            let ranked: Vec<snapback4_lang::Diagnostic> = ranked.into_iter().cloned().collect();
   447	            for report in note_reports(&ranked, true) { println!("{}", present(&report)); }
   448	        } else {
   449	            // Notes are advisory: ranked and grouped after the ok line, repeats shortened (note_rank).
   450	            let mut notes = reviewed.new.iter().chain(visible_security.iter().copied()).map(|note| {
   451	                let compact = completeness_note(note);
   452	                crate::note_rank::Note { site: note.site.to_string(), code: note.code.clone(), summary: present(&note_summary(note)),
   453	                    form: compact.is_none().then(|| crate::note_rank::compiled_form(&note.code, &note.rewrite)).flatten().map(|(lead, code)| (lead, present(&code))), compact }
   454	            })
   455	                .chain(certificates.iter().flat_map(|certificate| &certificate.search).map(|search| crate::note_rank::Note { site: search.site.to_string(), code: search.code.to_string(), summary: present(search.summary), form: None, compact: None }))
   456	                .collect::<Vec<_>>();
   457	            notes.sort_by_key(|note|(crate::note_rank::summary_order(&note.code),note.site.clone(),note.code.clone(),note.summary.clone()));
   458	            let cap=5.max(notes.iter().filter(|n|snapback4_lang::security::note_priority(&n.code)<=2).count());
   459	            let remaining=notes.len().saturating_sub(cap);notes.truncate(cap);
   460	            if remaining>0 {println!("{remaining} more actionable notes in check --verbose/JSON");}
   461	            for line in crate::note_rank::render(notes) { println!("{}", crate::presentation::abbreviate_quoted(&line)); }
   462	        }
   463	    }
   464	    if !options.json {security_reviews::print(&dispositions);}
   465	    if !options.json && (options.flags.contains_key("verbose") || options.flags.contains_key("cost")) {
//! Validate the complete command before any project, store or host work.
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000.000#4-cli-surface-contracts — admission, every synopsis and flag help share this command table.
use std::sync::OnceLock;
#[derive(serde::Deserialize)]
struct Flag { name: String, value: String, #[serde(default)] retired: bool }
#[derive(serde::Deserialize)]
struct Command { verb: String, positional: String, flags: Vec<Flag>, #[serde(skip)] allowed: String }
fn commands() -> &'static [Command] {
    static TABLE: OnceLock<Vec<Command>> = OnceLock::new();
    TABLE.get_or_init(|| {
        let mut commands: Vec<Command> = serde_json::from_str(include_str!("../../../packages/snapback4/bin/cli-commands.json")).expect("embedded command table");
        for command in &mut commands { command.allowed = command.flags.iter().map(|flag|flag.name.as_str()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" "); }
        commands
    })
}
fn command(verb: &str) -> Option<&'static Command> { commands().iter().find(|command|command.verb==verb) }
pub(crate) fn allowed(verb: &str) -> &'static str { command(verb).map_or("", |command| command.allowed.as_str()) }

pub fn validate(args: &[String]) -> Result<(), String> {
    let verb = args.first().map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("");
    // Pipe-owned private commands have positional protocols, not public flags.
    if matches!(verb, "--internal-witness-process" | "--internal-worker-watchdog") { return Ok(()); }
    let mut iter = args.iter().skip(1).peekable();
    while let Some(arg) = iter.next() {
        if arg == "--" { break; }
        if arg == "-h" { continue; }
        let Some(flag) = arg.strip_prefix("--") else {
            if arg.starts_with('-') && !arg[1..].starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit()) {
                return Err(if verb == "witness" { "E_INPUT: witness has an invalid flag; see guide witness".into() } else { format!("E_INPUT: {verb} takes no {arg}") });
            }
            continue;
        };
        let (name, inline) = flag.split_once('=').map_or((flag, None), |(name, value)| (name, Some(value)));
        if name != "help" && !allowed(verb).split_whitespace().any(|known| known == name) {
            if verb == "rollback" && name == "dry-run" { return Err("E_INPUT: rollback preview is --check; use rollback <dir> [--to N|latest] --check".into()); }
            // Witness arguments may contain bearer material even in a flag name.
            return Err(if verb == "witness" { "E_INPUT: witness has an unknown flag; see guide witness".into() } else { format!("E_INPUT: {verb} takes no --{name}") });
        }
        if switch(name) {
            if inline.is_some() { return Err(format!("E_INPUT: {verb} --{name} takes no value")); }
        } else if inline.is_none() {
            // Retired spellings route to their existing no-work explanation,
            // even with a missing value; new unknown flags still refuse here.
            if retired(verb,name) {
                if iter.peek().is_some_and(|value| !value.starts_with("--")) { iter.next(); }
                continue;
            }
            let Some(value) = iter.next() else { return Err(format!("E_INPUT: {verb} --{name} needs a value")); };
            // Search text is literal, including leading dashes and punctuation.
            if value.starts_with("--") && !matches!(name, "find" | "section") {
                return Err(format!("E_INPUT: {verb} --{name} needs a value"));
            }
        }
    }
    let has = |name: &str| args.iter().skip(1).any(|arg| arg == &format!("--{name}") || arg.starts_with(&format!("--{name}=")));
    if verb == "check" {
        if has("ack-security")!=has("reason"){return Err("E_INPUT: --ack-security ID requires --reason TEXT".into());}
        if has("ack-security")&&["ack-notes","fix","store","release","emit-lean","audit"].iter().any(|f|has(f)){return Err("E_INPUT: individual security review cannot combine with --ack-notes, --fix, saved-store/release, --emit-lean or --audit".into());}
        if has("release") && !has("store") { return Err("E_INPUT: check --release requires --store <path>".into()); }
        if has("as") && !has("residency") { return Err("E_INPUT: check --as requires --residency".into()); }
        let incompatible = if has("emit-lean") { Some(("--emit-lean", &["cost","residency","fix","verbose","store","fixture","as","release","ack-notes","audit"][..])) }
            else if has("release") { Some(("--store --release", &["cost","residency","fix","fixture","as","ack-notes"][..])) } else { None };
        if let Some((mode,flags)) = incompatible {
            if let Some(flag) = flags.iter().find(|flag|has(flag)) { return Err(format!("E_INPUT: check {mode} does not accept --{flag}")); }
        }
    }
    if verb == "serve" && has("wait-stopped") && args.iter().any(|arg|arg.starts_with("--") && !matches!(arg.as_str(),"--wait-stopped"|"--help")) {
        return Err("E_INPUT: serve --wait-stopped takes only a deployment directory; it does not start or signal a server".into());
    }
    if verb == "rollback" && has("bridge") && !has("check") { return Err("E_INPUT: rollback --bridge requires --check; it writes a bridge starter without activating it".into()); }
    if verb == "check" && has("audit") && ["cost","residency","fix","store","fixture","as","release","emit-lean","ack-notes"].iter().any(|f|has(f)) { return Err("E_INPUT: check --audit takes only --json or --verbose".into()); }
    if verb == "test" && has("verbose") && !has("residency") { return Err("E_INPUT: test --verbose requires --residency".into()); }
    if verb == "run" && has("token-env") && !has("release") { return Err("E_INPUT: run --token-env requires --release".into()); }
    if verb == "why" && has("instance") && crate::verbs::parse(args).positionals.first().map(String::as_str) != Some("recent") { return Err("E_INPUT: why --instance requires why recent".into()); }
    if verb == "token" && has("port") && has("release") { return Err("E_INPUT: token --port selects dev and cannot combine with --release".into()); }
    if verb == "token" && has("quiet") && has("json") { return Err("E_INPUT: token takes either --quiet or --json".into()); }
    Ok(())
}

fn retired(verb: &str, name: &str) -> bool {
    command(verb).is_some_and(|command|command.flags.iter().any(|flag|flag.name==name && flag.retired))
}
pub fn switch(name: &str) -> bool { name=="help" || commands().iter().flat_map(|command|&command.flags).any(|flag|flag.name==name && flag.value.is_empty()) }

#[cfg(test)]
mod integration_tests {
    use super::*;
    #[test]
    fn wave9_web_flags_and_upgrade_keep_shared_validation() {
        let args = ["witness", "web", "dist", "--url", "http://127.0.0.1", "--as", "alice", "--email", "alice@example.test", "--password-env", "PRIVATE_PASSWORD", "--mutation", "add", "--args", "{}", "--timeout", "30", "--export", "dist"].map(String::from);
        assert!(validate(&args).is_ok());
        for verb in ["upgrade", "witness"] {
            assert!(validate(&[verb.into(), "--unknown-wave9".into()]).is_err());
        }
        assert!(validate(&["upgrade".into(), "0.4.25".into()]).is_ok());
        assert!(validate(&["upgrade".into(), "--to".into(), "0.4.25".into(), "--json".into()]).is_ok());
        assert!(validate(&["witness".into(), "web".into(), "--password-env".into()]).is_err());
    }
}

fn value(verb: &str, name: &str) -> String {
    command(verb).and_then(|command|command.flags.iter().find(|flag|flag.name==name)).filter(|flag|!flag.value.is_empty()).map(|flag|format!(" {}",flag.value)).unwrap_or_default()
}

/// Generated from the same flag table used before dispatch and side effects.
pub(crate) fn help(verb: &str) -> String {
    let mut out = String::from("Flags (validated before any project or store work):\n");
    for name in allowed(verb).split_whitespace().chain(["help"]) {
        let value = value(verb, name);
        let description = match (verb, name) {
            ("deploy", "schema") => " — deploy a saved complete source; keep it outside snapback/",
            ("deploy", "staging") => " — classify a new disposable deployment as staging; existing production stores refuse",
            ("dev", "unsafe-dev-doors") => " — explicitly open unauthenticated local fixture/clock/persona doors for password-only apps",
            ("test", "source") => " — test a saved complete source; keep it outside snapback/",
            ("doctor", "repair-expiry-orphan") => " — offline recovery of one physically missing expiring parent, table:id; stop its host and back up first (guide expire)",
            ("clock", "each-boundary") => " — fire every crossed schedule window in chronological order (default: latest window only)",
            (_, "help") => " — print this verb's help (-h also works)",
            _ if retired(verb,name) => " — retired spelling; the command explains its replacement before work",
            ("doctor", "instance") => " — inspect one dev instance's health and refusals",
            ("doctor", "url") => " — inspect the backend at this URL",
            ("doctor", "latency") => " — measure request and stream latency",
            ("doctor", "reset") => " — reset latency observations before measuring",
            ("witness", "password-env") => " — environment variable holding the sign-in password",
            ("witness", "background") => " — run the native witness in the background; inspect witness status",
            ("witness", "if-present") => " — skip an absent native witness installation",
            ("witness", "environment-skip") => " — report an unavailable native environment as skipped",
            ("witness", "initialize") => " — initialize the witness installation",
            ("witness", "wait") => " — wait for the background witness to finish",
            (_, "json") => " — print machine-readable JSON on stdout",
            ("upgrade", "quiet") => " — print only per-project actions; suppress successful install/check/history output",
            (_, "fixture-only") => " — allow zero business cases for fixture loading and query probes",
            (_, "bridge") => " — with --check, write current source and this app's rollback checklist into a new directory",
            (_, "save-version") => " — save deployed sources, contemporary fixtures and immutable release label in a new directory",
            (_, "quiet") => " — print only the bearer token, with no successful stderr output",
            (_, "list") => " — list available guide cards",
            (_, "full") => " — print the complete guide card",
            (_, "sections") => " — print the card's numbered section index",
            (_, "section") => " — select a heading, topic or section number",
            (_, "find") => " — search every word (a|b or a OR b matches either)",
            (_, "as") => " — select a persona or principal for the operation/proof",
            ("dev" | "serve", "port") => " — backend port (0 chooses a free port; A-B tries an inclusive range)",
            ("init" | "new", "port") => " — backend port, 1–65535 (frontend defaults to the next port)",
            (_, "port") => " — running backend port, 1–65535",
            (_, "port-range") => " — inclusive range for backend and frontend ports",
            (_, "web-port") => " — frontend port, overriding the next-port default",
            (_, "bind") => " — listening IP address (default 127.0.0.1)",
            (_, "i-understand-cleartext") => " — explicitly allow a non-loopback HTTP listener",
            (_, "worker") => " — production worker entry path",
            (_, "long-waits") => " — held viewer request capacity (default 1024, max 4096)",
            (_, "long-waits-per-principal") => " — held viewer requests per principal (default 16)",
//! `check`'s advisory notes, ranked. A green check printed nine notes in
//! source order, and builders could not tell an FYI about offline coverage
//! from a page that never completes. Notes stay advisory (they never fail a
//! check); this only orders them, names each group, and shortens repeats.

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Debug)]
pub(crate) enum Tier {
    /// Can cost correctness or completeness: a page that never completes, a
    /// write no caller can pass, a disclosure, a read a device cannot answer.
    Review,
    /// Costs speed, meter headroom or offline coverage.
    Performance,
    /// What a declaration implies; nothing to fix for online use.
    Design,
}

pub(crate) fn tier(code: &str) -> Tier {
    match code {
        "N_IMMUTABLE_PATCH" | "N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL" | "N_UNREADABLE_PROBE" | "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER" | "N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT" | "N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED" | "N_MAINTAIN_AUTHORIZATION" | "N_WRITE_DENIED" | "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED" | "N_DELETE_NEEDS_READ" | "N_PATCH_CLEARS" | "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE" | "N_PROBE_ONLY_READ" | "N_VACUOUS_RULE" | "N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN" | "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED" | "N_UNIQUE_HIDDEN" | "N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE" | "N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN" | "N_REQUIRE_REFUSED" | "N_QUERY_FILTERED" | "N_SHAPE_METER" | "N_HELD_UNKNOWN" | "N_SEQUENCE_READ"
        | "N_SWEEP_DEADLINE" | "N_CLOCK_STORE_AHEAD" | "N_PUBLIC_AGGREGATE_COPY" | "N_MAINTAINED_COLUMN_DISCLOSURE" | "N_MAINTAIN_DELTA"
        | "N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT" | "N_COLUMN_RESULT_EXPOSURE" | "N_SEARCH_UNSCOPED" | "N_SEARCH_OPEN" | "N_TIMELINE_PROFILE_CAP" | "N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION" | "N_SELF_CLAIMED_RELATIONSHIP" | "N_CALLER_TIME" | "N_ROLE_WIDE_READ" | "N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE" | "N_ARGUMENT_GRANT" | "N_BROAD_READ" | "N_SECURITY_INTENT" => Tier::Review,
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#13.4 — a cursorless `first N` is what the declaration says: the first N rows (wave 3 #7).
        "N_SYNC_ORDER" | "N_NO_HORIZON" | "N_MAINTAIN_SYNCED" | "N_TIMELINE_COMPAT" | "N_NO_CURSOR" => Tier::Design,
        _ => Tier::Performance,
    }
}

/// Security findings that can grant authority stay ahead of repeated advice.
pub(crate) fn summary_order(code:&str)->(Tier,u8){
    let priority=snapback4_lang::security::note_priority(code);
    (if priority<=2 {Tier::Review}else{tier(code)},priority)
}

/// The first words of a note's line: what to do about it, or that nothing
/// needs doing (wave 7 #11: "put a one-line verdict on every advisory note").
// @ref LLP 3000.036#17.7 — the verdict leads; the explanation follows it.
pub(crate) fn verdict(code: &str, summary: &str, form: bool) -> &'static str {
    match code {
        "N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION" => "review: restrict authority assignment",
        "N_SELF_CLAIMED_RELATIONSHIP" => "review: verify the relationship before granting access",
        "N_CALLER_TIME" => "review: use server time or bound the supplied time",
        "N_ROLE_WIDE_WRITE" | "N_ROLE_WIDE_READ" | "N_BROAD_READ" => "review: confirm each admitted audience and row state",
        "N_INVARIANT_RESTATES_RULE" => "review: answer the audience questions from the spec",
        "N_SECURITY_INTENT" => "review: write independent intent from the spec",
        "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER" if summary.starts_with("persona-only:") => "no action without use identity",
        "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER" => "fix: add a production bootstrap writer",
        "N_FIXTURE_ARGUMENT_DRIFT" => "fix: update the fixture arguments",
        "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE" => "review: complete for the pinned parent audience; other callers may be capped",
        "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" if summary.contains("the complete form is to add `cap ") => "fix: add the exact source cap below, or accept `complete: false`",
        "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" if summary.contains("no verified complete form for this read") => "review: no verified complete form for this read; expect `capped` and show what arrived",
        "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" if form && summary.contains("the closest form is") => "fix: use the closest form below (one arm stays `complete: false`), or accept `complete: false`",
        "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" if form => "fix: use the complete form below, or accept `complete: false`",
        "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" => "fix: pin the scope, or accept `complete: false`",
        "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED" => "fix: declare the reference's `on delete`, or guard the delete",
        "N_DELETE_NEEDS_READ" => "review: some deleters get NOT_FOUND",
        "N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED" => "review: this reference argument has no mandatory target lookup",
        "N_MAINTAIN_AUTHORIZATION" => "review: hidden and absent are different caller outcomes",
        "N_PATCH_CLEARS" => "review: an omitted argument clears this column",
        "N_WRITE_DENIED" if summary.contains("admits only 'service:") => "review: a seat require grants no table authority",
        "N_WRITE_DENIED" => "fix: this write refuses E_RULE for callers you admit",
        "N_REQUIRE_REFUSED" => "review: callers you admit may still get E_RULE",
        "N_QUERY_FILTERED" => "review: this query branch may filter rows under table authority",
        "N_READ_DENIED" => "fix: this read always returns null",
        "N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN" if summary.contains("get NOT_FOUND") => "review: the hidden lookup runs before this require",
        "N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN" => "fix: this require cannot see the rows it tests",
        "N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE" => "fix: this nullable lookup is always null for the admitted callers",
        "N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN" => "review: a required read gate may hide rows from writers",
        "N_LOOKUP_FILTERED" => "review: confirm lookup authority and the hidden-row outcome",
        "N_UNIQUE_HIDDEN" => "review: hidden unique conflicts retain generic E_RULE",
        "N_PROBE_ONLY_READ" | "N_PUBLIC_AGGREGATE_COPY" | "N_MAINTAINED_COLUMN_DISCLOSURE" => "review: who can read this",
        "N_VACUOUS_RULE" => "review: who can write this",
        "N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT" => "review: confirm creation authority as well as edit authority",
        "N_COLUMN_RESULT_EXPOSURE" => "review: confirm every result/refusal audience",
        "N_IMMUTABLE_PATCH" => "review: readable conflicts require the original immutable value",
        "N_NO_CURSOR" => "no action for a top-N list",
        _ => match tier(code) {
            Tier::Review => "review",
            Tier::Performance => "no action unless it is slow (`check --cost` measures it)",
            Tier::Design => "no action for an online app",
        },
    }
}

fn heading(tier: Tier) -> &'static str {
    match tier {
        Tier::Review => "review — can cost correctness or completeness:",
        Tier::Performance => "performance — costs speed, meter headroom or offline coverage:",
        Tier::Design => "by design — what a declaration implies; no action needed for online use:",
    }
}

pub(crate) struct Note {
    pub site: String,
    pub code: String,
    pub summary: String,
    /// An expanded compiled repair: its lead line and the code. Compact
    /// completeness notes leave this empty and keep the repair in `--verbose`.
    pub form: Option<(String, String)>,
    /// Three-line default rendering for completeness verdicts. Their full
    /// planner proof and compiled repair remain in `check --verbose`.
//! Author-owned intent starts with a reviewable draft, never inferred policy.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#103-adoption — syntactic coverage is distinct from a proved invariant.
use super::*;

pub(super) fn missing(bound:&Bound)->Vec<String>{
    bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name,t)| {
        ([&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|!matches!(r,RuleExpr::Deny)) || !t.written_by.is_empty())
            && !bound.invariants.iter().any(|i| &i.table==*name)
    }).map(|(name,_)|name.clone()).collect()
}

/// Specification questions, never proposed enforcement or accepted policy.
/// The caller must keep this outside the compiled .q source set.
pub fn draft(bound:&Bound)->String{
    let mut out=String::from("-- DRAFT: NOT ACCEPTED. Answer from the APP SPECIFICATION, not from the rules.\n-- Each answer needs an audience, a resource relationship and lifecycle/consent conditions.\n-- Use table-read intent for a shared row audience; add column-read intent for distinct privacy boundaries.\n-- Assess every role and column, without automatically declaring one invariant for each question.\n-- Identify each role's positive product/workflow grant before retaining access to other people's private data.\n-- Write confirmed declarations in authored snapback/*.q, then run check -> test -> audit.\n-- This questionnaire is never compiled and does not propose policy.\n-- snapback4 guide security\n\n");
    let roles=super::intent::roles(bound);
    for (name,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){
        out.push_str(&format!("-- {name}: {}\n",super::intent::row_context(t)));
        let audiences=std::iter::once("a signed-in user without a roster role (check own rows and unrelated rows separately)".to_owned())
            .chain(roles.iter().cloned()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        for audience in &audiences {
            out.push_str(&format!("-- Q {name}.write / {audience}: may any user of this role write ANY {name} row, or only the owner of THIS resource? Which relationship and state must hold?\n"));
            out.push_str(&format!("-- Q {name}.read / {audience}: which rows may this audience see according to the spec? Own, assigned, team-wide, or none? Which state/consent conditions must hold now?\n"));
        }
        for (column,kind) in &t.columns {
            if column=="id"||t.engine_field(column){continue;}
            for audience in &audiences {
                out.push_str(&format!("-- Q {name}.{column} ({kind}) / {audience}: may this audience read this value, including downloads and derived values? On which related rows and in which states? If not, which independently confirmed column-read invariant excludes it?\n"));
            }
        }
        for inv in bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==*name&&i.column.is_some()&&i.target=="update"){
            out.push_str(&format!("-- {}: update-only intent excludes creation. Does the specification allow callers to supply this column at insert/upsert too? Use write only if creation shares the restriction.\n",inv.target_label()));
        }
        for effect in ["insert","update","delete"] {
            if covered(bound,name,effect){continue;}
            out.push_str(&format!("-- Q {name}.{effect}: who may {effect}, who appoints that authority, and which relationship/state must hold? Can callers claim the relationship themselves?\n"));
        }
        for (column,authority) in &t.written_by {out.push_str(&format!("-- Q {name}.{column}: may .{} edit this value in every state? Column update intent covers edits; write also covers creation.\n",authority.owner));}
        out.push('\n');
    }
    out
}

fn covered(bound:&Bound,table:&str,effect:&str)->bool{
    bound.invariants.iter().any(|i|i.table==table && i.operation.is_none() && i.column.is_none()
        && (i.target==effect || i.target=="write" && matches!(effect,"insert"|"update")))
}
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize)]
pub struct UncoveredExposure {pub table:String,pub effect:String,pub audience:String,pub row_predicate:String,pub column:Option<String>}
pub(super) fn uncovered(bound:&Bound,exposures:&[Exposure])->Vec<UncoveredExposure>{
    let mut out=exposures.iter().flat_map(|x|x.effects.iter().filter(|(effect,rule)|rule.as_str()!="deny"&&!covered(bound,&x.table,effect)).map(|(effect,predicate)|UncoveredExposure{
        table:x.table.clone(),effect:effect.clone(),audience:if predicate=="allow"||predicate.starts_with("public"){"every signed-in user".into()}else{format!("caller satisfying {predicate}")},row_predicate:predicate.clone(),column:None,
    })).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    for (name,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){for (column,authority) in &t.written_by{
        if covered(bound,name,"update")||bound.invariants.iter().any(|i|i.table==*name&&i.operation.is_none()&&i.column.as_ref()==Some(column)&&matches!(i.target.as_str(),"write"|"update")){continue;}
        out.push(UncoveredExposure{table:name.clone(),effect:"update".into(),column:Some(column.clone()),audience:format!("owner .{} = viewer",authority.owner),row_predicate:format!("field-owner patch authority for {column}; table update rule may deny")});
    }}out
}

## Summary

Snapback 4 is one of two parallel candidates for the successor to Snapback 2,
and the sibling of Snapback 3, not its successor. It builds
[LLP 2000.000](./2000.000-keep-the-core-flip-two-substrates.rfc.md) as
written: the Snapback 2 core kept verbatim, two substrates flipped up front —
resources metered at runtime instead of priced statically, and a client that
replicates the viewer's declared partition instead of caching receipted
results — proved first on the hub fixture, with a deterministic simulator as
the primary harness. Snapback 3 ([LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md), led by
Codex / GPT-6 Astra) keeps the same two questions open and discovers their
answers in its first slice. Snapback 2 ([LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md))
continues as the incumbent and the control. All three are judged by one
pre-registered comparison on one clock.

This document records the author's decisions of 2026-09-12 that created the
track, restates the bet on one page so a reader can orient without the brief,
fixes the bakeoff protocol, draws the fence, and plans the first four weeks.
The design argument and its evidence stay in LLP 2000.000. Only running code
will bind (`rules/RULES.md`).

## 1. Decisions (author, 2026-09-12)

On 2026-09-12 Charlie Cheever asked whether Claude Fable 5.1 should lead a
Snapback 4 while Codex / GPT-6 Astra leads Snapback 3 and Snapback 2
continues, "and see which one turns out best." The lead answered yes with
conditions; the author accepted them ("Sounds good") and asked for this
document. The decisions below are therefore the lead's proposal as accepted
by the author. Where the author has not separately ruled, the text says so.

1. **Three tracks, one comparison.** Snapback 2 continues as the incumbent
   and the control. Snapback 3 builds LLP 2000; Snapback 4 builds LLP
   2000.000. This is a bakeoff of *bets* — discover the substrates in the
   slice versus decide them first — not of models. The result will say more
   about the bets than about the leads, and §3 is written so that it can.
2. **The name is Snapback 4.** Siblings, not a sequence: "4" does not mean
   "after 3." A separate number gives the track its own corpus root,
   directory, package identity and data directory with no rename when the
   bakeoff resolves, and files never move in LLP. If Snapback 4 loses, it is
   a number that never shipped. "Snapback 3b" was considered and rejected:
   it reads as a variant of 3, and this is a different bet.
3. **Snapback 4 builds LLP 2000.000 as written.** Its §14 open questions are
   the lead's to decide inside the track, recorded in §7 as they are
   decided. The author rules on the bar, the comparison and promotion (§3).
4. **One owner on the core.** The lead writes the engine, the store and the
   client contract. Codex lanes build the fixture generator, the reference
   applications, the device witnesses and the periphery. Blind reviews from
   two model families are reserved for the two disclosure-sensitive
   mechanisms: partition inversion of read rules (LLP 2000.000 §7.1) and
   the meter's cut behaviour on non-uniform scans (LLP 2000.000 §6). This
   departs from the standing rule that Codex codes and Claude orchestrates,
   deliberately, for the core only.
5. **The clock is four weeks** from the first commit under `snapback4/` to
   the first slice running on the hub fixture. At the clock the author kills
   the track, merges its substrates into a survivor, or continues it. There
   is no third month without a ruling.
6. **Shared and not shared.** Shared with Snapback 3: the LLP corpus, the
   Clique harness and scorers, the fixtures, the briefs, and the simulator
   once either track has one. Not shared: code, except the Snapback 2 pieces
   both are expected to copy (LLP 2000.000 §5.1).
7. **Snapback 2 keeps shipping and freezes new substrate work for the
   duration.** Fix-forward, the loop bar, acquisition and papercuts continue;
   no new certificate axes, client custody mechanisms or ceiling changes.
   This is accepted here as a condition of the bakeoff. Making it binding on
   concurrent Snapback 2 sessions needs one line in `rules/NOT-DOING.md`'s
   Moves ledger, which this document does not write.
8. **Reading load.** This root, updated in place, and one append-only
   journal, `snapback4/JOURNAL.md`. Sub-LLPs only for a decision that must
   outlive the journal. No panels beyond decision 4's two reviews.

## 2. The bet on one page

**Keep verbatim from Snapback 2** (LLP 2000.000 §5.1): the rule language
with its uniformity and hoist analysis, `decided_true` and the
structural-fact refusals; one data language, Quarry, with `--` comments,
`require … else CODE`, `shape`, `merge`, `walk`, `chunk`; the value model
and its conformance corpus; typed read and write states with no silent state
and the refusal envelope `{code, family, site, message, rewrite, guide}`; the
successor classifier (widening additive, checked narrowing, exact projection
ownership); `deliver`'s engine-computed audience, tombstones and
`bootstrap`; `schedule`, `job`, `chunk` and rehearsal; personas; `init`,
`dev`, `check`, `doctor`, `run`, `test`, `why`, `trace`, `guide`; H0
`deploy`, `rollback`, `secret`; channels; assets; the word-prefix index.

**Flip 1 — certify authorization, meter resources** (LLP 2000.000 §6). The
certificate keeps every read site's shape, uniformity, completeness class
and prediction eligibility, and stops pricing bytes and steps. A
deterministic meter charges examined rows, index entries, rule probes,
steps, heap, output and rows written as the program runs, on the server and
in the client interpreter alike. A query cut by the meter returns `capped`
with no continuation past the cut, the same observable outcome as a static
ceiling cut today; a mutation aborts with `E_BOUND` naming actual work; a
job is chunked. `check --cost` prints the structural cost class; `test`
prints measured meters on the hub fixture.

**Flip 2 — the viewer's partition is the unit of sync** (LLP 2000.000 §7).
`sync to <audience> last N by <index>` on a table makes the engine maintain,
per principal, the rows that principal may read, as a change log with a
horizon; a table without the word is online-only, and `check` says which
rule term prevented inversion. The client holds a replica of its partitions,
one watermark each, a durable outbox with client-minted ids, and the
interpreter; queries run locally and are `complete` inside the horizon.
Revocation is a scope tombstone; `until-revoked` and `delivered-history`
survive as the client's leave policy. Two client tiers — online-only
without a store, local-first with one — share one language and one server.

**The vocabulary is fixed before the mechanism** (LLP 2000.000 §8):

```ts
type Read<T> = { data: T; complete: boolean; fresh: boolean; since?: number; next?: Cursor }
            | { loading: true } | { denied: Refusal };
row.pending: boolean;
type Write = { state: "pending" } | { state: "sent"; seq: number } | { state: "failed"; why: Refusal };
type Link = "online" | "offline" | "signed-out";
```

A screen that needs a word not on that card is a defect in the mechanism,
not a gap in the card.

# LLP 2000.000: Snapback 3 — keep the core, flip two substrates, start at hub scale

**Type:** RFC
**Status:** Draft
**Role:** Second opinion on LLP 2000; since 2026-09-12 also the design brief Snapback 4 builds (LLP 3000)
**Systems:** Snapback 3 product architecture, bounded computation, client data model, client API vocabulary, language and tooling, effects runtime, verification (simulation), performance budgets, process
**Author:** Claude Fable 5.1, at Charlie Cheever's request
**Date:** 2026-09-12
**Revised:** 2026-09-12 (adopted as the Snapback 4 design brief by LLP 3000 decision 3); 2026-09-12 (initial)
**Related:** [LLP 2000](./2000-snapback3.rfc.md) (parent), [LLP 3000](./3000-snapback4.explainer.md) (the Snapback 4 root that builds this brief), LLP 0000, [LLP 0329](./0329-agent-native-rebuild-diagnosis.research.md), [LLP 0329.014.004](./0329.014.004-bounded-data-programs-v1-design.rfc.md), [LLP 0329.016](./0329.016-agentic-dev-loop-from-scratch.rfc.md), [LLP 1000](./1000-snapback2.explainer.md), [LLP 1001](./1001-query-engine.spec.md), [LLP 1002](./1002-dev-loop.spec.md), [LLP 1012](./1012-local-first-v1.spec.md), [LLP 1018.003](./1018.003-truthful-and-composed-client.decision.md), [LLP 1018.008](./1018.008-elegance-after-the-corrections.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.007](./1018.008.007-affordable-offline-acquisition.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.008](./1018.008.008-locality-of-application-changes.research.md), [LLP 1018.008.009](./1018.008.009-residual-ergonomic-gaps.research.md), [LLP 1019](./1019-derived-delivery-custody.rfc.md), [LLP 1020](./1020-day-two-evolution-and-legible-ceilings.rfc.md), [LLP 1023](./1023-clique-builder-jump.rfc.md), [LLP 1024](./1024-single-lane-delivered-search.rfc.md), [LLP 1025](./1025-digest-final-emission-at-hub-scale.research.md), `rules/RULES.md`, `rules/NOT-DOING.md`, `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-05-clique-full-3x3/holistic-scores.md`

## 1. Summary

LLP 2000 is right that the whole application is the unit of design, right
that ownership comes before abstraction, and right to ask for deterministic
simulation and an early real deployment. Its weakness is that it treats the
two structural choices that produced Snapback 2's costs as open research
questions, and then proposes a first experiment at exactly the scale where
those costs are invisible. This document takes a position on both, names
what to keep from Snapback 2 verbatim, and rewrites the first slice so it
can fail for the right reasons.

The position, in one paragraph. Snapback 2's *core* is excellent and should
be copied: the rule language and its uniformity analysis, the structural-fact
family, one data language, typed states with no silence, refusals that paste
a rewrite, personas, the successor classifier, the derived per-recipient
log, the loop verbs and their sub-second budgets. Two *substrates* under
that core are where the record shows the hours went, and both should be
flipped rather than re-investigated: (1) the static certificate should keep
certifying **authorization and shape** and stop pricing **bytes and steps**,
which move to a deterministic runtime meter with honest `capped`/abort
outcomes; and (2) the client should hold **a replica of the viewer's declared
partition** — the local-first shape every shipped sync engine and Snapback
2's own `deliver` log converge on — instead of a per-query result cache
proven by receipts. Those two flips dissolve most of the machinery that
leaked into the application (receipts, coverage, halos, validators, grants,
ID slices, drains, `withHistory`, follower inboxes, per-table retention
words) and turn the 28-second acquisition into a bulk stream whose floor is
the device's write speed. The first slice should be built on the **hub
fixture** (1,000 memberships, 500 follows, 100,000 messages), with the
day-two change designed before the day-one screen, a native client in the
first slice, and a deterministic simulator as the primary test harness.

Three claims this document makes that LLP 2000 does not:

1. **The costs have two named causes, and both are decisions, not
   mysteries.** Byte-priced static certification and the receipt-proven
   result cache account for every ergonomic residual in LLP 1018.008.009
   except documentation. §2 shows the mapping.
2. **The first experiment must include the hub.** Snapback 2 was "elegant on
   day one and hostile on day two" (LLP 1020) and broke at 1,000 memberships
   (LLPs 1023, 1024, 1025). A two-principal slice will pass and teach nothing.
3. **The process is part of the product.** Seventy-one LLPs, over two
   thousand commits, and seven ceiling raises in fourteen days produced a
   sound system nobody can hold in their head. Snapback 3 needs one owner on
   the core, a simulator instead of a test matrix, and budgets anchored to
   competitors so that raising one means admitting a loss.

## 2. What the record says, read as causes

The evidence LLP 2000 §2 cites is right; its reading is too gentle. Every
row below is a Snapback 2 cost recorded in the corpus, with the substrate
that produced it. Numbers are the corpus's own and retain its scope limits.

| Recorded cost | Where | Cause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Adding `editedAt: time ?` (44 bytes × 244 retained copies = 10,736) pushed `messages.searchAll` to 4,199,688 closure bytes against 4,194,304; the builder shrank a page from 20 to 19 | LLP 1018.008.008; 0.0.21 day-two diary | **Static byte pricing.** Closure bytes are a bound on what a receipt could ship; the product paid for the bound, not the work |
| The 1,000-membership digest fold certifies at 9,922,698 heap and 8,691,704 closure bytes and is refused; the recipient-keyed form is admitted at 2,733,253 / 1,848,418 | LLP 1025 §3 | **Static byte pricing** (heap = steps × 32 + closure scratch). A runtime meter would have run the fold and reported actual bytes |
| A message page is 50, not 200, because a row "prices at 16,016 bytes" and 200 exceed the 1 MiB output ceiling | LLP 1012 §3 | **Static byte pricing** of the worst-case row width |
| A per-recipient search projection is refused at 20,863,641 reserved steps against 1,000,000 | LLP 1024 §1 | **Static step pricing** of a derivation the engine would perform incrementally |
| Full acquisition of 10,008 messages: 67.4 s → 53.6 s → 32.6 s → 28.4 s against a 2–3 s objective; 201 requests, 200 acknowledgement mutations, 612 IndexedDB transactions; PowerSync reports 10,000 rows in 0.36 s native and 2.5 s on web | LLP 1018.008.007; LLP 1018.008.009 §4 | **Result cache, not replica.** Every 50-row page is a certified query, a receipt, a durable acknowledgement mutation, and a store swap |
| Adding a count to a thread makes the composite `partial` offline whenever count authority is missing, so the template declares an independent `history` query and `withHistory` bindings; 27 files / 1,718 lines → 28 / 1,751 after the fix | LLP 1018.008 F2; LLP 1018.008.009 §2 | **Result cache.** The count is not a fact the receipt covers, so the client cannot compose locally |
| `shared/thread.ts` describes table identity, row selection, order, create membership, draft fields and mutation targets a second time | LLP 1018.008 F2 | **Result cache.** Items must be reconstructed from answers because there is no local table to read |
| Sending stalled with 85 IDs remaining against a certified 352-ID slice on an 8,192-ID grant; recovery needed a "Sync now" action | LLP 1018.008 F1 | **Result cache + engine-minted IDs.** Prediction needs server ids before commit, so ids are granted in slices |
| A follower browser tab writes through a durable inbox row; the SDK carries `forwarding`, `writer-unresponsive`, `inbox-full`, `follower` states | LLP 1018.004 | **Result cache.** One writer per partition image; other tabs cannot mutate a receipt-proven image |
| Global search over 1,000 memberships costs ~1,010 HTTP requests through a client N-way merge | LLP 1024 §2 | **Result cache** (no local index over the partition) + **static pricing** (the server-side projection is refused) |
| The complete local runtime grew 7.8 KB → 89,843 gzip bytes; the ceiling was raised 34,000 → 50,000 → 66,000 → 82,000 → 82,500 → 84,000 → 85,000 → 90,000 | LLP 1000 §"Revised"; `rules/NOT-DOING.md` §Moves; LLP 1018.008 §10 | **Result cache.** The inputs list of `metrics/twin.jsonl` is coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, posting trees, fact integrity, swap journals — receipt machinery, not the interpreter |
| `Snapshot` carries `data, next, partial, receipt{state, source, seq, next, reason, rule, sites}, freshness{live, staleSince, connection}, local{predicted, retained, caughtUp, watermark}, refusal, status, reason` | LLP 1018.003 §1 | Both. Every field is a true fact about the mechanism; almost none is a fact about the application |
| `Schema::rule_hash` binds every queued write to the complete schema; an optional column on the subject table invalidates unrelated evidence | LLP 1018.002; LLP 1018.008.008 | **Result cache.** Evidence identity exists because held answers must be re-authorized; a replica re-runs the mutation at the server and needs no evidence identity |
