//! Review questions describe enforcement; they never infer specification intent.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#131-intent-and-diagnostics — advisory comparisons do not change proof statuses or authority.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::{schema::{ColumnType,Table},Value};

pub(super) fn roles(bound:&Bound)->Vec<String>{
    let mut roles=BTreeSet::new();
    for (name,table) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){
        let mut columns=super::privilege::role_columns(bound,name);
        // Scoped memberships have the same questionnaire need as global roles.
        // Require an observed caller probe; an event's `kind` plus `author`
        // is not enough to invent a role roster.
        for (_,other) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){for rule in super::columns::rules(other){
            let mut parts=Vec::new();leaves(rule,&mut parts);
            for part in parts{if let RuleExpr::Exists{table:roster,index,key,equalities,..}=part&&roster==name{
                let pairs=super::privilege::probe_pairs(bound,roster,index,key,equalities);
                if pairs.iter().any(|(c,v)|matches!(v,RuleTerm::Viewer)&&matches!(table.columns.get(c).map(ColumnType::required),Some(ColumnType::Principal))){
                    columns.extend(pairs.iter().filter(|(c,v)|matches!(c.as_str(),"role"|"kind")&&matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(_)))).map(|(c,_)|c.clone()));
                }
            }}
        }}
        for column in columns {for value in super::privilege::role_values(bound,name,&column){
            roles.insert(format!("{name}.{column} = '{value}'"));
        }}
    }
    roles.into_iter().collect()
}

pub(super) fn row_context(table:&Table)->String{
    let mut relationships=Vec::new();let mut states=Vec::new();
    for (column,kind) in &table.columns {
        match kind.required(){
            ColumnType::Principal=>relationships.push(format!(".{column} (principal)")),
            ColumnType::Ref{table}=>relationships.push(format!(".{column} ({table})")),
            ColumnType::Enum{variants}=>states.push(format!(".{column}: {}",variants.join(" / "))),
            _=>{},
        }
    }
    format!("relationships: {}; state/category values: {}. Confirm their meaning from the spec.",
        if relationships.is_empty(){"none declared".into()}else{relationships.join(", ")},
        if states.is_empty(){"none declared".into()}else{states.join(", ")})
}

/// Bounded distribution retains each AND condition when showing OR audiences.
/// Oversized formulas stay whole; no branch or condition is silently dropped.
fn alternatives(rule:&RuleExpr)->Vec<RuleExpr>{
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>{
            let out=xs.iter().flat_map(alternatives).collect::<Vec<_>>();
            if out.len()>32{vec![rule.clone()]}else{out}
        },
        RuleExpr::And(xs)=>{
            let mut out=vec![Vec::new()];
            for x in xs {
                let next=alternatives(x);
                if out.len().saturating_mul(next.len())>32{return vec![rule.clone()];}
                out=out.into_iter().flat_map(|prefix|next.iter().map(move |part|{
                    let mut parts=prefix.clone();parts.push(part.clone());parts
                })).collect();
            }
            out.into_iter().map(RuleExpr::And).collect()
        },
        other=>vec![other.clone()],
    }
}
fn leaves<'a>(rule:&'a RuleExpr,out:&mut Vec<&'a RuleExpr>){match rule{
    RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for x in xs{leaves(x,out)},
    RuleExpr::Not(x)=>leaves(x,out),r=>out.push(r),
}}
fn audience(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr)->String{
    let mut actors=BTreeSet::new();
    fn collect(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,actors:&mut BTreeSet<String>){match rule{
        // Negated role tests are conditions, not an admitted role.
        RuleExpr::And(xs)=>for x in xs{collect(bound,x,actors)},
        RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..}=>{
            let pairs=super::privilege::probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
            if !pairs.iter().any(|(_,v)|matches!(v,RuleTerm::Viewer)){return;}
            let columns=super::privilege::role_columns(bound,table);
            let mut named=false;
            for (c,v) in &pairs{if columns.contains(c)||matches!(c.as_str(),"role"|"kind"){
                if let RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(value))=v{actors.insert(format!("{table}.{c} = '{value}'"));named=true;}
            }}
            if !named{actors.insert(format!("a caller with a matching {table} relationship"));}
        },
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,term)|RuleExpr::Eq(term,RuleTerm::Viewer)=>{
            actors.insert(format!("a caller satisfying {}",RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,term.clone()).quarry()));
        },_=>{},
    }}
    collect(bound,rule,&mut actors);
    if actors.is_empty(){"a signed-in caller satisfying this branch".into()}else{actors.into_iter().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ")}
}

pub(super) fn questions(bound:&Bound,target:&str,effect:&str,gate:&RuleExpr)->String{
    alternatives(gate).iter().filter(|branch|kernel::implies(branch,&RuleExpr::Deny)!=Ok(true)).map(|branch|{
        let actor=audience(bound,branch);
        let mut fields=BTreeSet::new();branch.fields(&mut fields);
        let image_fields=branch.any_term(&|t|matches!(t,RuleTerm::OldField(_)|RuleTerm::NextField(_)));
        let scope=if fields.is_empty()&&!image_fields{"no row relationship or lifecycle condition in this branch".to_owned()}
            else if fields.is_empty(){"row-image conditions shown in the branch".into()}
            else{format!("row conditions on {}",fields.iter().map(|f|format!(".{f}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))};
        format!("{}Rule {target}.{effect} admits {actor} under `{}` ({scope}). Should the spec allow this audience on these rows and columns, including other people's rows and every lifecycle state?",if matches!(effect,"insert"|"update"|"delete"|"write"){format!("May any {actor} write any {target} row, or only the owner of THIS resource? ")}else{String::new()},branch.quarry())
    }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" ")
}

/// Lint-only normalization. It is never used by the proof or a counterexample.
/// Named-key probes with extra equalities imply the same probe with fewer
/// equalities; an existing row's self-id probe repeats its own current fields.
fn normalized(bound:&Bound,target:&str,current_read:bool,rule:&RuleExpr)->RuleExpr{
    match rule{
        RuleExpr::And(xs)=>RuleExpr::And(xs.iter().map(|r|normalized(bound,target,current_read,r)).collect()),

    fn project_insert(&self, info: &TableInfo, row: Expr) -> Expr {
        if info.decl.columns.iter().any(|column| column.written_by.is_some()) || !self.schema.tables[&info.decl.name].column_reads.is_empty() {
            Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::ProjectFields { table: info.decl.name.clone() }, args: vec![row] }
        } else { row }
    }

    fn write_result_type(&self, info: &TableInfo) -> Ty {
        if super::columns::fields(info).next().is_some() { Ty::WriteRow(info.decl.name.clone()) } else { Ty::Row(info.decl.name.clone()) }
    }

    fn calendar_number(&self, value: &A, depth: u8) -> bool {
        if depth > 8 { return false; }
        match value {
            A::Call { name, .. } => matches!(name.as_str(), "day" | "hour" | "bucket"),
            A::Name { name, .. } => self.binding(name).is_some_and(|binding| binding.revision == 0 && binding.origins.len() == 1 && self.calendar_number(&binding.origins[0].1, depth + 1)),
            A::If { then, otherwise, .. } => self.calendar_number(then, depth + 1) || self.calendar_number(otherwise, depth + 1),
            _ => false,
        }

   383	pub(super) fn run(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
        "N_VACUOUS_RULE" => "review: who can write this",
        "N_INSERT_WRITE_INTENT" => "review: confirm creation authority as well as edit authority",
        "N_COLUMN_RESULT_EXPOSURE" => "review: confirm every result/refusal audience",
        "N_IMMUTABLE_PATCH" => "review: readable conflicts require the original immutable value",
        "N_NO_CURSOR" => "no action for a top-N list",
        _ => match tier(code) {
            Tier::Review => "review",
            Tier::Performance => "no action unless it is slow (`check --cost` measures it)",
            Tier::Design => "no action for an online app",
        },
    }
}

fn heading(tier: Tier) -> &'static str {
    match tier {
        Tier::Review => "review — can cost correctness or completeness:",
        Tier::Performance => "performance — costs speed, meter headroom or offline coverage:",
        Tier::Design => "by design — what a declaration implies; no action needed for online use:",
    }
}

   312	    let transitions=bound.programs.iter().filter(|bp|bp.program.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation&&!bp.program.native&&!bound.schema.operations.get(&bp.program.name).is_some_and(|f|f.ephemeral)).filter(|bp|{
   313	        ir::write_tables(&bp.program).iter().any(|(name,kind)|relevant.contains(name)&&(*kind!="insert"||bound.invariants.iter().any(|i|&i.table==name)))
   314	    }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
   315	    if transitions.is_empty(){return;}
   316	    let Ok(initial)=readback(fixture)else{return;};let actors=actors(bound,&initial);
   317	    if !out.reserve(1){return;}
   318	    let Ok(root)=fixture.fork_fixture()else{return;};
   319	    let mut frontier=vec![(root,Vec::<Step>::new())];
   320	    fn signature(bound:&Bound,rows:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>)->String{
   321	        // Equality of this projection is only an exploration heuristic. Missing
   322	        // worlds are explicitly uncovered; it never discharges an obligation.
   323	        let projected=rows.iter().map(|(name,rs)|{let t=&bound.schema.tables[name];let mut images=rs.iter().map(|r|t.columns.iter().filter(|(c,k)|c.as_str()!="id"&&matches!(k.required(),C::Enum{..}|C::Bool|C::Principal|C::Ref{..})).filter_map(|(c,_)|r.field(c).ok().map(|v|(c.clone(),v.clone()))).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>()).collect::<Vec<_>>();images.sort_by_key(|r|serde_json::to_string(r).unwrap());(name.clone(),images)}).collect::<BTreeMap<_,_>>();serde_json::to_string(&projected).unwrap()
   324	    }
   325	    let mut seen=BTreeSet::from([signature(bound,&initial)]);let mut visited=0;
   326	    for _ in 0..8{
   327	        let mut next=Vec::new();
   328	        for (base,steps) in frontier{
   329	            let Ok(before)=readback(&base)else{continue;};
   330	            for bp in &transitions{
   331	                let p=&bp.program;
   332	                let targets=ir::write_tables(p);
   333	                if targets.iter().all(|(t,_)|!super::super::privilege::role_columns(bound,t).is_empty()){continue;}
   334	                if targets.iter().all(|(t,kind)|*kind=="insert"&&before.get(t).is_some_and(|rs|rs.len()>=2)){continue;}
   335	                let cases=fixture_cases(bound,p,&before,&actors,out);

codex
**NOT READY.** Seven material findings against LLP 3000.036.004 §16:

1. **[P1] A branch-local guard can suppress the upload gate for unguarded creation paths.**  
   [creation_advice.rs:46](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-sec7/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/creation_advice.rs:46)

   Suppression uses the first creating write’s context for every write in the mutation. If the first branch contains `require photo != null` before inserting, but another branch inserts with optional `photo` unguarded, `nonnull(first.context)` suppresses the gate everywhere. Excluding `$advice` guards does not exclude explicit requirements nested inside a conditional branch.

   **Repair:** Track requirements that unconditionally dominate all creating paths, including helper expansion. Add a regression with guarded first-branch creation and unguarded alternate creation; the gate must remain pending.

2. **[P1] Default check labels new security obligations as performance advice.**  
   [note_rank.rs:133](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-sec7/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/note_rank.rs:133)

   Selection uses `summary_order`, but `render()` subsequently sorts and groups by the old `tier()`. Most new SEC7 codes—including both gates—fall through to `Tier::Performance`. Their fallback verdict is **“no action unless it is slow”**, and they can appear after result inventories or restating questions. This contradicts the gate and likely-hole presentation contract.

   Check also still sorts stringified sites lexicographically at [verbs.rs:457](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-sec7/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/verbs.rs:457), unlike audit’s numeric ordering.

   **Repair:** Use the shared security priority throughout selection, rendering, headings and verdicts; preserve numeric site components. Test actual default CLI output with a gate, an advisory question, result inventory, restate, and lines 41/101.

3. **[P2] Insert-result handling erases cross-table disclosure lineage.**  
   [result_advice.rs:44](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-sec7/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/result_advice.rs:44)

   The new insert arm replaces the inserted variable’s existing flow with destination-table lineage. For example, after a public query omits `secrets.secret`, a mutation doing `return insert copies {value: s.secret}` previously retained `secrets.secret` as an alternate observer. It now retains only `copies.value`, potentially eliminating the exposure note entirely. The new `ProjectFields` handling at line 24 independently discards its argument’s lineage.

   **Repair:** Merge destination-row identity with existing field provenance, and propagate provenance through `ProjectFields`. Add cross-table copy-and-return regressions with and without destination column rules.

4. **[P2] Retained-role matching can mistake the assigner’s role for the recipient’s.**  
   [creation_advice.rs:20](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-sec7/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/creation_advice.rs:20)

   `bound_role()` accepts the recipient appearing in **any** probe key, then substitutes it with `viewer`. Consider a roster keyed uniquely by `principal`, with additional `assistant` and `role` columns:

   `require exists roles[viewer, role = 'manager', assistant = recipient]`

   This checks the assigner’s manager role and identifies their assistant; it does not require the recipient to be a manager. Nevertheless, the detector accepts the literal role atom and can impose `N_RETAINED_ROLE_GRANT`.

   **Repair:** Resolve key/equality columns before substitution and verify that the roster’s principal binding is the stored recipient. Keep original caller and recipient identities distinct during analysis. Add this companion-principal-field negative control.

5. **[P2] Directed histories silently exclude relevant role mutations and boolean-only workflows.**  
   [histories.rs:310](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-sec7/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/histories.rs:310), [histories.rs:333](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-sec7/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/histories.rs:333)

   Exploration requires a non-role enum somewhere in the relevant tables, then unconditionally skips mutations whose targets are role tables. Thus a required three-step history such as `open → revoke role → finalize` cannot be explored by the directed frontier even when the invariant explicitly depends on that role. The breadth/pair campaign cannot generally recover a three-mutation witness. Boolean-only mutable eligibility histories are excluded at entry.

   These exclusions also receive no specific uncovered reason.

   **Repair:** Admit relevant mutable role, boolean and relationship dependencies alongside enum transitions; include creator-check dependencies in relevance. Report deliberate exclusions explicitly. Add a regression requiring three mutations with a role change in the middle.

6. **[P2] The frontier limit does not bound retained fixture copies during expansion.**  
   [histories.rs:371](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-sec7/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/histories.rs:371)

   Every accepted successor retains a complete `Engine` in `next`; truncation happens only after expanding every current world and operation. Twenty distinct successful mutation paths already exceed the stated sixteen-world maximum. Larger campaigns can retain hundreds of database/asset-ledger copies before truncation.

   **Repair:** Maintain the bounded priority beam incrementally, dropping displaced engines immediately with deterministic tie-breaking and explicit coverage reporting. Verify peak retained-world count, rather than only the final frontier length.

7. **[P2] Role-wide-write suppression treats unrelated text as an ownership relationship.**  
   [creation_advice.rs:67](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-sec7/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/creation_advice.rs:67)

   Any condition containing both `viewer` and `.` suppresses the question. With a separate global-role requirement, a condition such as `o.status = 'pending' or viewer = 'service:operator'` therefore counts as a resource relationship, although it establishes no ownership. Dots inside literals can also satisfy this test.

   **Repair:** Match resolved viewer-to-target relationship comparisons structurally, respecting branch dominance. Add negative controls for unrelated state checks, service alternatives and dotted literals.

Static review only; no tests were executed and no files were changed.
